ParlaMint-AT_2004-01-29-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00046_d7e3410	It was necessary, Mr President, first of all, for us to have taken a path of rehabilitation: to consolidate the budget in order to have the possibility of a financial margin in order to be able to counter-tax. We also see that the educational allowance or the educational premium has a very positive effect, as is the research allowance and the research premium. One of the special advantages of the Federal Republic of Carinthia is the early depreciation of buildings. As this early depreciation is provided for in economics packages I and II, and Carinthia is a special tourist country, we also see increased effects for the Federal Republic of Carinthia due to the high share of building investment. Secondly, with regard to the first stage of the tax reform, which introduced the half-average rate of tax on profits not collected: This is a very important capital formation measure especially for the many small and medium-sized enterprises in tourism in Carinthia. In other words, these measures have been very useful for the tourism country of Carinthia. Another example: By producers such as Infineon, i.e. in the field of microelectronics, the research allowance and the education allowance have been very positively accepted.

The Rules of Procedure here state very clearly that the member of the government in question is obliged to reply. However, he/she can refuse this answer for various reasons – legal, data protection, political, factual – but must state these reasons. I believe that we are in favour of this response for good reason. As regards the content of the answer: There is no right of assessment and no right of choice of the President. In the absence of such an advantage, the founder cannot be subject to an income tax duty. A gift tax does not apply if the fund serves non-profit or generous purposes. This is the result of § 15 para. 1 Z 14a of the Heritage and Gift Tax Act.

So far, great successes have also been recorded in the reduction of noise. Thus, since 1970 noise has fallen to half, and since 1976 noise has declined steadily. However, traffic noise – both during the day and at night – is still the most commonly mentioned source of noise. In this context, road traffic disruptions, while rail and air transport are only of local importance. The cost of noise protection measures on roads and railways has therefore increased sharply in recent years. However, the rehabilitation of the entire road and rail network will continue for a longer period of time. For example, noise protection walls and lärmarm road coverings with a total length of 728 kilometres have been used on federal roads since 1983, and more and more noise protection windows have been used. From 1990 to 2000, around €180 million was invested in the protection of noise on the roads and buildings of the Federal Republic of Germany. It is interesting to note that from 1992 onwards, expenditure on noise reduction has gradually decreased. Only with the government taking office under our Federal Chancellor Dr. Wolfgang Schüssel did these investments begin to rise again. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] Without a doubt, the need for noise protection devices has increased as a result of the increase in traffic. In the years 2000 to 2003, a sum of around €80 million was invested for extensive protection measures. For this year, € 48 million is budgeted, and for the coming year, this figure will rise to € 52 million. This project comprises an investment volume of 159 million €. By September 2003, this significantly reduced the noise burden for 140,000 rail inhabitants. Despite all the measures in this area, it is in no way possible to forget planning and construction. In this way, local development planning needs to be more and more tried to achieve a spatial separation of heavily run roads and residential areas. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] With us in Vorarlberg, for example, spatial planning has tried to reduce individual shopping and leisure activities, such as by strengthening the local cores or by providing appropriate regulations for shopping centres outside the settlement area. In recent years, urban and rural bus transport has also been set up in the country. Noise control is a cross-sectional matter in Austria and is governed by the jurisdiction of the federal or state. At European level, the Environmental Noise Directive has now taken an important step towards a level playing field. The previous efforts have already made great success in the area of noise protection, and with the budgeted resources available, I am convinced that further noise reduction measures can be put in place so that Austrians can continue to sleep in the future. Nevertheless, there are successes in the fight against noise in our country. Noise reduction and protection measures have reduced noise interference by 50 percent since 1970. This is also a factor that can be seen. How much was used to combat noise in Austria between 1990 and 2000? – 180 million € were made available for noise protection on roads within the Federal Republic of Germany. In 1999, the limit of 65 decibels per day and 60 decibels per night was reduced by 5 decibels. This also means that the built-in noise protection measures in the past can often no longer meet these requirements. Hubert Gorbachange has already been addressed here. The financing of extensive noise protection measures along the existing motorway and highway network is planned. It was €31.5 million in 2003, €48.3 million in 2004, and €52.1 million in 2005, according to ASFINAG's statements and investment planning. As regards the railway noise: The rail traffic disaster has been in place since 1993/94. The projects planned and completed by September 2002 in this area comprise an investment volume of € 159 million. This made it possible to achieve a dramatic reduction in noise pollution for approximately 140 000 passengers on railway lines. As far as the noise in Austria is concerned, these data also give rise to hope. Despite a marked increase in flight movements, air traffic noise has not increased. Why? – Because especially the loud Chapter 2 aircraft are banned at all Austrian airports with the exception of Vienna during the day. The share of the new noise generation of Chapter 3 aircraft is constantly increasing, and it is also crucial that, despite increasing flight movements, the noise pollution in aviation in Austria could be kept half constant. Nevertheless, we are not satisfied, but we are working intensively on this – I have already referred to the division of competences – that we are implementing the European Union Environmental Noise Directive accordingly. What is the aim of the directive? – The definition of a common European approach to reducing harmful environmental noise impacts on people through common methods developed and through consistent implementation in the Member States. Ladies and gentlemen! It is necessary to implement a number of technical measures at local level. In this sense, I am already conducting the first intensive coordination talks with the countries, with the various ministries, the BMVIT, BMWA and so on in the coming month to discuss the implementation and uniform procedure on the issue of the European Union Environmental Noise Directive.

ParlaMint-AT_2004-01-29-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00046_d7e10329	Mr President. Federal Minister! High house! We today have two proposals from the Committee on Justice, which regulate a very important area, an area whose importance will be much greater in the future. Let me mention a few figures: Currently about 70 000 people live in Austria in about 800 old and nursing homes, and currently about every fifth citizen is over 60 years old, but already in a few decades, in 2030, every third citizen will be over 60 years old. It is therefore particularly important to ensure appropriate legal certainty in this area, through regulations that have not yet been established. This has taken some time because there have also been some competence problems, and it has been necessary to clarify between the competences of the federal government and the countries. But in the end, in two different areas, which I would like to present to you very briefly, we have succeeded in creating such rules. These two areas are, on the one hand, civil law, civil legislation in the so-called Home Treaty Act, and on the other hand, freedom-restricting measures and their regulation in the Home Residence Act. I would like to address these two areas very briefly. Home contract law has been re-regulated by a number of additional paragraphs in the Consumer Protection Act. Provisions have been introduced which have not yet been made in this area, which are intended in particular to protect the inhabitants of the home and the future inhabitants of the home. For the first time, therefore, this proposal provides for mandatory rules for the home contract on, for example, premises, equipment, meals, but also on the benefits of the sickness of residents, on the amount of the payment and on the termination of the home contract. In addition, this is also particularly important and was inserted in the Committee on Justice by means of an amendment request, in the future this Home Treaty Act of Home Contracts also calls for findings on the area of personality rights, which are essential. The Home Treaty is intended to provide for how the home wants to ensure these personal rights and their guarantee. Important regulations in the Consumer Protection Act are the binding writtenness of the home contract, but also rules on a deposit which is at all events required and on the termination and possible dissolution of the home contract. The second area, as I have already mentioned, is the so-called home residence law, which has been created for the first time and in which regulations and regulations are made for the first time for restrictions on freedom, which unfortunately are always necessary, in order to protect the inhabitants themselves. In particular, these include legal certainty for both areas, for the staff of nursing homes and for those concerned who in the future have clear rules on the conditions under which such restrictions of freedom may take place, when they need to be terminated, who should be ordered and how control should be ensured when such restrictive measures become necessary. It is very important to specify the condition here, when any restriction of freedom may take place at all. This too has not yet been regulated in any way and is now quite clearly expressed. There must be a mental illness or mental disability. This disease or disability must seriously endanger the life or health of the person concerned or of other people in this home, and it must be the only means to prevent such a risk of health. However, it is also regulated, as has been said, who is allowed to lay down restrictions on freedom in the future, and above all, that such measures can only be maintained for so long as the reason for this measure exists and must be terminated immediately if this reason has been removed. The control is provided for in the form of a binding automated legal representation. It was particularly important to us, especially my group, that the right of self-determination of the resident who lives here is maintained, because first and foremost, the legal representative will be the one whom the home resident or the home resident has, on his own initiative, established as such. This can be a close relative, but it can also be a lawyer or notary. Only if the home resident has not made a provision in advance will a legal representation, by the Association for Stakeholder and Patient Prosecutorship, ensure that, in the event that such freedom-restriction measures are necessary, they too are protected accordingly. In the final analysis, however, it is also provided for the possibility of a review by the court, which may be requested by the person concerned or by his legal representative at the court. I believe – and I believe that this is the essential thing in these two regulations – that, as I mentioned earlier, a very, very important area for the future, of which one often does not like to speak or hear, really future-oriented regulations have been created. I should therefore like to thank the Lord. Federal Minister, especially his employees in the department who were involved, thank you for this draft. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] It is clear that these regulations, as we will decide today, will not allow cases, as we have heard them again in recent months from Vienna, to be excluded by a hundred percent, but that they will provide legal framework and legal certainty for all sides. Nevertheless, the main responsibility for decent, decent, decent treatment and care will continue to lie in particular with the home bearer, who will have to ensure that there is sufficient financial and human resources for the homes in order to avoid such cases, as we unfortunately know them repeatedly from Vienna, in the future. – Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-AT_2004-01-29-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00046_d7e11099	Mr President. High house! When I heard Dr. Jarolim, I believed I was on a false steamer. I hope that the SPÖ will also agree with these laws, because from my point of view these laws, which we decide today, are a milestone in legal certainty, especially for our elderly and in need of care. Therefore, please do not criticise things that will certainly make a significant improvement for everything! The People's Procuratorate has already lodged a home treaty law, even several times, in their reports it is to be read. I've been allowed to talk about this before. And thanks to the knowledge of the Constitutional Court mentioned by the Federal Minister, the situation of competence was finally clarified so that the legal provisions could now be formulated. From my point of view, it is very useful to separate the two laws, because the Home Treaty law regulates many more homes and affects much more older people than the Home Residence Act, so that the number of persons concerned makes sense. In addition, they are completely different legal instruments of competence. The Home Treaty Act is based on civil law competence in accordance with Article 10(1). 1 Z 6 B-VG, while for the Home Residence Act Article 10 para. 1 Z 12 B-VG, i.e. health care, is important. A mixture would certainly not have been useful here. In principle, I must say that we already have a very good example in the field of home contract regulations, namely the Student Home Act of 1986, which has really brought a significant improvement and also a detachment of the relationship between home governments and home residents. It can be seen that a clear legal system has a very, very good effect. Above all, when it is clearly regulated what is required in the Treaties, a balance is made between the home carrier and the person who is dependent on the home site. The Consumer Protection Act has been chosen from the possibilities to regulate this. One could certainly have taken the ABGB, perhaps one could have made a special law. At any rate, all that is required from our point of view was placed in these laws. Since it is said again and again that it is not required for appropriate prices, I should like to point out: Please, that's just not federal competence, but that's national competence! [[Abg. May. Johann Maier: That's not true!]] Here the law would be threatened by the abolition. However, it has been stated in the current law that charges must be broken down by accommodation, catering and special services. This regulation alone will lead to a clear cost transparency. We have also, although it has been difficult to regulate this competence, brought in the personal rights – I would say almost: through the back door – by having to include in the home contract also, as the personal rights are preserved, for example, the right to visit, the protection of the telecommunications secret, on your own choice of doctor and also the right to take away your own furniture – if, of course, this is possible according to the circumstances of the home. In any case, this must be regulated by the Home Treaty. Of particular importance are the following: first of all, the writtenness, because it creates legal certainty; the freedom of charge is certainly a very essential point, because it makes it clear that no additional costs are incurred; the possibility of having trustees in order to have a higher protection; the reduction of the fee in the performance of the home carrier; the maximum amount of the deposit has been fixed, as well as the termination by the home resident – and also the termination by the home carrier, for example, because the latter also has a right to receive the payment. He finally has to pay for the staff, the electricity, the gas and so on. So there must be a balanced relationship here, and the current rules offer this balanced ratio. The new law applies to all contracts starting from 1. It was completed on 1 July 2004. However, in a resolution, the Committee on Justice has expressed its hope that, at a reasonable time, all existing treaties will also be converted to these priorities. I am sure that the Minister of Justice will also look to see if the experience of the time will give rise to a need for innovation, which will certainly be met accordingly by the judicial body. In this sense – I can express it here again – we particularly welcome the new Home Treaty Act. It is a milestone in legal certainty for our older people!

ParlaMint-AT_2004-01-29-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00046_d7e12665	Mr President. High house! During the debate by Mr Brinek, the question was asked: What's the message? – I would like to give you supplementary education and tell you what the message is. [[Abg. Dr. Jarolim: We ask for this! You're profound enough!]] The message is that here in the House we have adopted a good university law 2002 which forms the basis for the largest university reform that has ever existed in Austria, which is internationally recognised. [[Abg. Dr. Jarolim: With laughter it becomes international...!]] The second message is that we have thus created good conditions for universities with which they have great opportunities in the field of teaching and research. The third message is that this law is constitutional. 54 provisions challenged by you have been clearly confirmed. The University Council, the appointment of the University Council, the Senate, the Rectorate, the cooperation, the own organisation plan, all the issues of study law – everything was clearly confirmed. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] This decision gives the university the security to implement the new university law in a targeted manner. And I really want to thank you here: The universities work perfectly. 139 university councils were appointed, 21 rectors were appointed, all vice-presidents were appointed, the Senates were set up, and the SAP system for the conversion of the budget was introduced by the university members. – A great thank you to all those who work so hard! Now to the budget, which is constantly being criticized. – universities actually have 6 percent more available in 2004 than in 2003. To this end, they receive extra funds for research infrastructure, for preferential professorships, for necessary construction measures, for special courses, for research scholarships, and they receive significantly more money from the Austrian Federal Government's research offensive. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] The fact that university conditions are good shows that we have a record of students, more students than ever – 31 950 – and the total number of students has increased by 3 percent. That is, who wants to study in Austria, who has the skills, can also study, because our scholarship system is so pronounced that those who need it get support. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] Now on the individual questions. Question 1: “How is the student participation in the University Law 2002 regulated?” I mean, it's no longer up to date that you have 100, 200, 280 bodies in which advice is given, but there should be clear skills and qualitative participation – a qualitative participation that meets the requirements of the 21st century. ==References== The participation of the students we have included in the university law is as follows: A participation as a partner of universities, in the management principles for universities and in the performance of their duties is enshrined in the University Law. In the Senate, the university's board of directors, the students are like before – and I ask you to take note of this! – as before, as at the UG 93, represented with 25 percent of Senate members. Students also have at least one quarter of the members in the college bodies for the adoption of the study plans. The student representatives are represented in vocational and habilitation committees. The courses are evaluated, and the university has the obligation to involve students in their further planning, i.e. to respond to the evaluation of the courses. Students are entitled to decide for the first time on the use of the study contributions at their universities. Further improvements we have made are: Improvements in the scholarship system, for working students, for students with child care obligations. ==References== In January 2004, students will be able to drop their study contributions, no matter what they are studying, for the first time through the first stage of tax reform. So we have made numerous improvements for the students. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] On question 2: “What rights does UG standards 2002 for each individual student? “ Each student has the legally guaranteed right to four examinations. Each student has the legally guaranteed right to three examination dates per semester. A negative assessment of the last review does not lead to a permanent exclusion from the study. The legal protection of examinations is still fully guaranteed in the University Law 2002. These regulations are in accordance with the University Organisation Act 1993 and have continued to be incorporated into the University Law 2002 at the request of the student representatives. Question 3: “What was the participation of the ÖH in the open planning process in the creation of the UG 2002?” We have worked according to the method of open planning. We have set up a university platform. This university platform has been held seven times. Of the seven times before the ÖH election took place, students' representatives were regularly present. At that time, the AG had the chair, the executive was. As the red-green majority subsequently took over the executive, the student representatives only came to two out of four platforms and did not make any contribution there. We then tried very hard to get further into the conversation with the students, did more events and conducted discussions. And we have also taken into account the main demand of student representatives: These were co-determination in study issues – taking into account the evaluation with consequences – taking into account three examination dates per semester – taking into account four legally guaranteed examinations; Legal certainty in the case of audits – also taken into account. We have therefore received the requests of the student representatives. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] Question 4: “After the last ÖH elections, how does the executive of ÖH come together at the federal level and at the University of Vienna? “ The result of the high school elections in May is the following right to vote: 184 498, valid votes 53 792, voting party 29.9 percent. [[Abg. Brosz: Is the subject of execution?]] The executive of the ÖH at the federal level is composed of: Green Alternative Students – 14 Mandates, Association of Socialist Students of Austria – ten Mandates. The result of the ÖH election at the University of Vienna is: The University Office is composed of: Green Alternative Students – nine mandates, Association of Socialist Students – six mandates, Communist Student Association – two mandates. On the last question, on question 5: “How do you appreciate the events described and the choice of words of those involved?” [[Abg. ParlaMint-AT_2004-01-29-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00046_d7e19356	We now come to points 9 to 11 of the agenda on which the debate is being held under one. There was no oral reporting. Mr Fekter was the first speaker to speak. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2004-01-29-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00046_d7e19380	Mr President. Mr. Minister! High house! Agenda items 9, 10 and 11 are summarised in the debate, so I would like to briefly explain a few things, since they are very different. Agenda item 9 concerns an adoption Convention of 1965. This adoption Convention has only a very small scope of application because it is limited to certain Member States. At the same time, it has been replaced by the Hague Convention on the Protection of Children and Cooperation in the field of International Adoption. We therefore have a much better international legal basis, and for this reason we have decided to terminate this old adoption agreement. The two agenda items 10 and 11 are quite different; these are both economic laws. Agenda item 10 on the Commercial Code concerns the re-regulation of liability limits for auditors and the postponement of the entry into force of certain strict limits of liability by two years. The same is the case in the Banking and Insurance Supervision Act in item 11. There too, the legislative measure is merely a delay in the date of entry into force, for example for external rotation. This has therefore become necessary, ladies and gentlemen, because, in the run-up to this, serious concerns have been expressed as to whether such high limits of liability are useful in the market if insurance cannot be concluded in this respect, because there are no reinsurance, or vice versa, whether the external rotation is actually efficient in the control of balance sheet manipulations. There is only one country in which the external rotation is in legal power, and that is Italy. Due to the Parmalat scandal, it is clear that the external rotation does not prevent balance sheet manipulation. In the course of this debate, of course, there have also been concerns that these individual measures, i.e. liability provisions and rotations, cannot help the auditor to achieve an excellent quality of the balance sheet work, the control of the figures and the so-called economic hygiene. That is why we have documented the political will of the Federal Government in a very comprehensive resolution. We want to improve the quality of auditors, remove incompatibilities, and it should not be possible for self-control. It is not possible to draw up the balance sheets in advance and then, as a auditor, to make a decision about this numbering work again. We also want to intensify measures against insider trade. We want to improve the reliability of financial information. This economic hygiene package will be discussed this year and will be put into effect by next year at the latest. We need time for this, and for that it was necessary to postpone the legislative adjustments to the Banking Act and the Commercial Code a little further, namely by the end of 2005. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2004-01-29-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00046_d7e19945	We now come to points 12 and 13 of the Rules of Procedure on which the debate is being held under one. There was no oral reporting. The first speaker was Mr Wittmann. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2004-01-29-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00046_d7e19969	Mr President. Mr. State Secretary! High house! In itself, this eGovernment law is a right law in the goal. There is a fundamental consensus that such a law should be introduced as quickly, widely and quickly as possible. It is therefore a pity that the very well-founded views of the experts in a long hearing here in Parliament have not been taken seriously by the relevant concerns and recommendations and have not tried to build them up. . I just want to express these main concerns here. One of the most fundamental concerns about this law is that it is completely unreadable and therefore it will be difficult to find acceptance in individuals accordingly. It is not even legible for legal experts, because it includes a matter that is legally difficult to understand, because it is mostly technically to interpret. The regulation formulated in this law is therefore too complicated because the adoption of such a scheme depends on how easy access is and how simple the appropriate possibilities are. This is not guaranteed in this case! One could have made this much easier: One could have simply continued to work with the existing law on electronic signature. You have a working solution at the Ministry of Finance. However, this functioning solution will now be transferred to this complicated scheme after five years or until 2008. I do not know why the existing simple rules, which one could have maintained with a very simple law, are combined into a complicated matter, which, in addition – that is the second concern – becomes far too expensive, because it is our own delivery services, if you want to get in touch with the authorities. Imagine the citizen who has contact with this authority once or twice a year and who has to sing and pay for this at a professional electronic delivery! This is certainly in order for lawyers or tax advisers, but only this entry barrier will make the acceptance of this law appear very, very questionable. Another problem is that the delivery shifts the risk from the level of administration to the individual. As it is now, in the event of a longer absence, the issuing authority carries the risk, in the future the risk of individuals, i.e. the citizen himself. This is absolutely not civil-friendly! Moreover, access for disabled people is not at all available. The relevant EU directives are not complied with. In fact, however, this law should apply to all if they come into contact with authorities. It is a pity that the concerns mentioned have not been addressed, because it would have been easy to reach a basic consensus. – I believe that it should be a common basis for the law-making that a law is made easier, cheaper and applicable to all. The reason for the complexity of this law is that you are using a central service provider who ultimately gets a key via the sector-specific identification numbers, which can be reproducible at any time with this service provider. Once this key is manufactured, it is reproducible there. And this service provider is comically the Ministry of the Interior, which at the same time also has to use the grid suspension as instruments. There is a conflict of interest when this law stipulates that this service provider is the Ministry of the Interior! With this in mind, I ask myself: Why is this database created at all? Why does the path not end in the respective Department Department? Why don't you actually use a private service provider, and why does it have to be the Ministry of the Interior if you don't want to connect this data sometime? I don't think that's necessary in this law. This shoots far beyond the goal! And, of course, it is also extremely worrying from the point of view that, in the end, those who organize the search for data are also made the key keeper of all the data of the ministries. – I think this is extremely worrying, and I believe that we will be able to cope with many, many problems. Constitutional concerns have also been expressed by independent professors who have also examined this. This law also intervenes in the administrative organisation of the countries and thus also in country competences. This is why there is, of course, constitutional need if these regulations are to be implemented in a simple federal law and not in constitutional provisions. That's a shame, because the basic approach would be correct and desirable. However, these regulations are too complicated, too expensive, constitutionally worrying, the risk of delivery is shifted from the administrative authority to the individual, access for the disabled is not guaranteed, and this association of data in the Ministry of the Interior is quite worrying. I would therefore propose that a uniform and better system be established, that this matter be discussed again in the Constitutional Committee and that complaints and recommendations be taken seriously. We are therefore submitting a referral request to the Constitutional Committee. We would be able to deliver a perfect law in four weeks because everyone is interested in maintaining a cheaper, more uncomplicated and easily accessible e-government law.

ParlaMint-AT_2004-01-29-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00046_d7e20486	Mr Morak, State Secretary, spoke from the Government Bank. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2004-01-29-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00046_d7e20505	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! Ladies and gentlemen! I am very sorry about Mr Wittmann's speech because, at the outset, especially in the Committee's discussions, I felt that there is a sense of the problem on the one hand and that there is also the courage to go with us on the other. I think that what is available to Parliament today – what we have presented – is a modern, innovative and courageous law. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] It brings added value to the administration, added value to citizens and also to a slimmer state. It allows more communication in both directions, more security for each one of us. We have talked about data protection on one occasion or another, and you will certainly remember that in the Committee, especially by the otherwise more critical Professor Thienel, no data protection concerns were expressed, but quite the contrary. [[Abg. May. Johann Maier: But constitutional concerns!]] No worries were expressed! [[Abg. May. Johann Maier: He expressed constitutional concerns!]] – In the field of data protection, I am sure not, Mr Deputies. [[Abg. Dr. Jarolim: Mr State Secretary, you demand courage to be unreasonable!]] I can also tell you that the security measures we have taken in the data area are being discussed, ladies and gentlemen, the identification, i.e. the citizen card, also as a reference model in the EU bodies. Of course – and I would like to say that too – the process, as this law was made, is part of what speaks for the good of this law. We started the consultations in 2001 and presented the concept of the specific identification of persons. Since the beginning of May, cooperation has taken place in the eGovernment working groups of the countries, so there has been a very close networking of the cities, the municipalities, the federal government and the countries. The introduction of a first overall concept in 2002 took place in March, and in the middle of 2002 the first drafts followed, and so on, and so on. ==References====External links== In December 2003, with the end of the three-month standstill period in the notification procedure. Ladies and gentlemen! What is it about in this law and what does this law express? – The parameters are first the citizen card that regulates the law, the electronic identification, the authenticity and identification with the electronic signature. The second is the sector-specific person label, which provides an exact identification on a data protection basis, and third, the standard document register with the electronic detection of personal data. As we have heard from representatives of the A-Trust, each individual citizen in Austria has between ten and 15 passwords, which he naturally, because he cannot remember them all, either write them down or he formulates them in such a way that they are easily available, with his name, with his first name, with the name of his children and so on. This is not possible in this area. A citizen card has been created which clearly and surely allows the identification of each individual to the Authority, but also in e-banking or e-billing. The standard document register, ladies and gentlemen, is the proof of ever-needed data, i.e. the birth certificate, the address, the citizenship certificate, and allows what we call one-stop shop. These include, for example, optimizations in the existing reporting obligation with the People's Status Authority and the Citizenship Authority. The area-specific person labels are labels that are not consistently comprehensible, so that all data are not always available, but ensure that only those areas that are related to each other are questioned. This is preventive data protection with an intelligent technology. The law has provided numerous improvements in the sense of civil kindness. As the costs are always mentioned here: Ladies and gentlemen! A certificate for the safe signature including the citizen card function costs between 10 and 12 €. A card reader – a one-time investment – € 20; 50 percent of it is refunded. A former registration fee of 10 € will be added. How does the citizen – and this too has been carried out in our expert questioning hour – come to a secure signature card with the citizen card function? Remember that this year four million banknote cards will be issued! By 2006 6.5 million banknote cards will be issued. Exchange takes place every three years. So you can always use the latest technology here. On all these bank cards there is a slumbering, secure signature that you just have to retrieve. Where can you get that? – For example, one can call it in the banking offices, the notaries, the lawyers, the Telekom shops and the Austrian Chamber of Commerce, ladies and gentlemen. So it's not witchcraft to activate the citizen card function. If I may provide you with further examples, I have already mentioned this in committee: If we request a notification today, this requires a written request, and after three to four weeks you will receive the notification. Cost: 15,10 €. With the citizen card you get the same over the Internet, at the computer enabled, now in two minutes. Cost: 3 €. So with a single registration request, you have the cost of the signature in here. It has also been ensured that for people who do not have access to the Internet, the municipalities or district authorities authorise organ managers for those affected, so that applications can be made in the ordinary procedure. Of course, all these processes are still possible in paper form, the transitional period runs until 2008. In this sense: It has already been said that significant progress has been made in this offensive for eGovernment 2003/2005, especially last year. It has already been mentioned the place one of Austria in the development and growth of eGovernment, the place four in the overall ranking of the eGovernment base services and two in the full interactive eGovernment offers. In principle, of course, we also believe that modern public administration must not stop before the use of modern communication technologies. We agree on this. There are a large number of state tasks that can improve the contact between citizens and state, but above all there are two issues that need to be considered: This is, on the one hand, data protection and data security and, on the other, easy and equal access for all citizens. It's just that there's a risk of abuse. Remember what has already happened, such as the transmission of data sets, the illegal sale of reporting data by the Ministry of the Interior and so on. There are therefore a number of data protection problems that cannot be completely eliminated by the government template. Above all, the fact that the Data Protection Commission is at the same time the Authority in operational management and control body is problematic. – In other words, it would be theoretically possible for the Data Protection Commission to determine against itself. So there are a number of problems, especially the expert hearing, and there are a number of questions, such as why the law on electronic signatures deviates from the federal law. So obviously the signature law did not prove to be successful, otherwise it could have been novelled. The question of why all authorities are forced to switch to this complicated procedure from 2007 onwards has also remained open. The concern that a database can be created at the same time from the Central Reporting Register, in which all the files in the area of the authorities can be technically linked and it will be possible to access them, and that the Federal Ministry of the Interior is the authority which is also technically able to achieve and control this database, which is already a very serious matter. It is to be asked why, especially in the case of such sensitive matter, there is no clear separation between the executing authority, i.e. the master number register authority, and the data protection authority, the data protection commission. Especially these concerns had almost all the experts who were present at the expert hearing, although all were aware of the need for e-government. However, almost everyone has demonstrated that the issues of security and data protection have not been satisfactorily resolved. For example, Professor Thienel pointed out that constitutionality was not everywhere. Mr Sacher of the ARGE data, for example, said that the law was overly concerned with the problem of identification, that security would be better guaranteed if it were guaranteed by different systems and that the system affected human dignity because it was objectively suited for monitoring. – That’s just a point you don’t get over. Mr. Einzinger of ISPA, the association of Internet providers, has assembled that this is a personal identification law, that it is in fact a law of administration for administration and that it would have been better to adapt the signature law. For all these reasons, we reject this eGovernment law – not because we want to close ourselves to the need for a modern administration, but because, for the reasons mentioned above, the protection of the citizen is not guaranteed and the existing proposal – which has already been noted in the writing of the proposal – is mainly based on the interest of the administration and not on the interest of the citizens.

ParlaMint-AT_2004-01-29-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00046_d7e21683	Mr President. Estimated Secretary of State! High house! This law will save enormous costs, as we have already heard, and will save citizens and authorities many ways in the future. Therefore, the ÖDP is not in vain committed to this law in a special way, because it will also result in Austria receiving one of the most modern government administrations in Europe. As the expert statements have been addressed several times today, I would like to say that we all know: three experts – five opinions! So, you can see the glass half full. I would like to stress once again in my speech the benefits of this new law, after my previous speakers have already given very detailed attention to the various aspects of the law. If I repeat on many points, I would just like to underline the importance and accuracy of these statements. The E-Government Act creates the conditions for the introduction of a new citizen card, a standard document register for the electronic detection of important personal status and other data as well as the possibility of electronic delivery of administrative documents. This creates the legal basis for the e-Justice between citizens and the authority. What are the benefits of this, especially for citizens? – Documents that you have had to take with you again and again with each application will be left at home in the future. Documents such as birth certificates, citizenship certificates or commercial certificates are stored in the standard document register and no longer need to be presented. In the future, on the private computer and in the office or on other terminals, it will be possible for any citizen to make official applications, to issue decisions and to be transmitted electronically. The often hourly employment of authorities – which is very well known – will be a thing of the past. Dear ladies and gentlemen! In this context, it has been criticised that this only helps citizens who are familiar with computers, or it is only helpful if the district authorities or municipalities offer this modern administration, because the eGovernment law only provides for the voluntary introduction. To this end, I would like to say: The fact is, that 42 percent of Austrian households already have Internet access. Online connection with the World Wide Web is constantly increasing. As we know, we have also promoted this tax by creating the possibility of tax deductibility of broadband technology. But our children also learn how to deal with the computers in the lower classes of schools, and more and more seniors are interested in courses on computers or how to deal with the Internet. For persons who do not have access to the Internet, services are provided at the local authorities. I would also like to stress that the use of electronic commerce is free of charge. High house! I would like to say with these statements that in a few years, almost every citizen will be able to gain access to the eGovernment, and any authority will offer this opportunity. We are the first country in Europe to offer the simplest, fastest and most cost-effective administrative procedures. We can be proud of that! We have also provided the conditions for access to the disabled to be accessible. Ladies and gentlemen of the opposition! The objective of the eGovernment is not to monitor or ‘glass’ people, the objective of the eGovernment is to ensure secure communication between the applicant and the authority. To this end, we have installed enough safeguards in eGovernment to prevent abuse. High house! This law will allow for electronic traffic, and we will be a pioneer in Europe as a whole. I am sure that the eGovernment will prove itself and all the other countries of the continent will follow us in this area, not to say: we will also be envied for this! – Thank you very much for your attention.
